      │   ├── skill_preflight.py ← Heal-safe read-only payload preflight: manifest/syntax checks plus registration-aware literal UI-schema resolution; unresolved dynamic schemas are explicit degraded skips, with runtime validation still fail-closed
      │   ├── project_journal.py ← Thin per-project journal/workpad tools (v6.32.0): journal_write/read (durable milestone memory), workpad_read/write (scratch page), journal_tail_digest (context injection); over-limit writes are rejected, never silently sliced
      │   ├── presence.py ← `configure_presence` owner bindings/selections, `initiate_presence` proactive cycles, and intrinsic typed completion/correlated-cancel tools
      │   ├── task_tree.py ← (v6.38.0) Task-tree coordination tools tree_note/tree_read (the swarm blackboard + child→parent beacons; storage/kind SSOT in ouroboros/task_tree_ledger.py)
      │   ├── followup.py ← `schedule_followup`: one deferred follow-up written into the EXISTING supervisor scheduled-task table (`state/scheduled_tasks.json`) — exactly one trigger is required: one-shot `{"type":"once","run_at":ISO}` or recurring 5-field cron with optional timezone; the supervisor tick enqueues ordinary root tasks under normal admission; agent-authored verbatim objective (no host template), typed refusals (delegated subagents may not mint future root tasks; cap 2 pending per task; over-limit text refused, never truncated)
      │   ├── join_ledger.py ← Soft-join decision authority: validates direct lineage and exact current child-result hashes for tagged `tree_note(kind="decision")` dispositions (`integrated`, `irrelevant`, `deferred`) — single-child or batch `children` array form, each batch entry validated individually — appends the sole authoritative task-tree row, rejects stale hashes as `CHILD_RESULT_STALE`, and keeps `peek_task`, `discard_child_result`, constraint override, cancellation, and shared child-decision helpers.
      │   ├── delegate.py ← Nanny verb facade: exact `delegate_start(subagent_id=..., prompt=...)` / idempotent `retry_of`, event-only `delegate_wait` with optional one-shot reasoned checkpoint, custody-gated `delegate_cancel`, and typed `delegate_answer`. (`delegate_start(prompt="")`, `subagent_runtime.exact_start`, `skill_payload`, `delegate_supervision.py`, `delegate_recovery.py`, `delegate_custody.py`, `gateways/claudexor.py`)
      │   ├── delegate_integration.py ← The C1 integration seam of the nanny verbs (extracted from tools/delegate.py for the module-size gate; delegate.py re-exports it so sibling code and tests keep one name): `_mutation_authority` derives the unified host authority record `{target_root, source, capture_mode}` for a mutating run (acting write_root vs B5 external-workspace root, typed refusals on any disagreement), `_provision_snapshot` registers + describes the private execution snapshot durably BEFORE any start intent, `_resolve_retry_invocation`/`_validated_invocation`/`_retry_binding_refusal` rebuild a retried start from its ONE durable invocation record and re-prove the C1 binding (pre-C1 mutating rows refused; moved workspace refused; GC-collected snapshot refused), and `_capture_terminal_patch` idempotently captures a terminal mutating run's diff from its snapshot for explicit `integrate_delegated_patch` disposition. (`_payload_mutation_authority`, `authority_source="skill_payload"`, `ResolvedResourceBinding`, `resource_ref`, `_rebind_payload_reference`, `_write_payload_patch_artifacts`, `blocked_reserved_paths`, `integrate_payload_patch`, `request_extension_reconcile`)
      │   ├── subagent_integration.py ← integrate_subagent_patch: parent's manifest-first integration of an acting subagent's workspace.patch. (`coop_already_in_tree`, `coop_checkpoint.checkpoint_commit_coop_roots`)
      │   └── patch_verdict.py ← The ONE writer of a patch apply/reject verdict, extracted from `subagent_integration.py` at its 1600-line ceiling (which re-exports it under the historical private name `_write_verdict`, so every import and monkeypatch target survives): one coherent WRITE concern shared by BOTH patch pipelines — `integrate_subagent_patch` and `integrate_delegated_patch`, whose verdict subject is minted as `run_<rid>` and classified here rather than by prefix-matching in readers. (`delegate_run_patch_verdict`)
      ├── delegate_start_claims.py ← One short pre-transport transaction for a fresh actor start: it serializes the final zero-run/custody recheck and `START_REQUESTED` append, nesting the existing exact-payload claim only when that resource is selected; transport and waiting stay outside every claim lock.
      ├── process_containment.py ← `ProcessContainer` for the hermetic gate: env-token membership (`OURO_PROC_CONTAINER_*`, /proc environ on Linux, `ps -E` on macOS, kill-on-close Job Object on Windows), read from LIVE kernel state at reap time; an alive or undeterminable member is an honest hard-block answer, never a kill guarantee.
      ├── platform_layer.py ← Cross-platform process/path/locking helpers, including the public descendant-enumeration seam reused by launcher cleanup and the Windows Job Object ABI with explicit argtypes/restype
      └── node_runtime.py ← Execution-probed Node runtime health (`node_runtime_health`: runs `node --version`, memoized by (path, mtime, size); a missing binary is never cached, and a timeout verdict is cached with its probe budget and re-probed only by a larger one) plus the skill-family policy `select_skill_node_runtime` (bundled-first with health rollback to PATH) and `skill_node_emergency_path_dir`. platform_layer lazily re-exports the public names for compatibility

      ouroboros/process_custody.py ← Supervised spawning + durable orphan ledger
      (v6.26.0): `spawn_supervised()` records every long-lived child in
      `data/state/process_ledger.jsonl` ({pid, pgid, fingerprint{start_time,
      cmd_sha256}, purpose, scope task|session|daemon, owner_task, session_id});
      the reaper (server startup + 10-min supervisor tick) kills entries whose
      generation/task owner is gone, matching by STRICT fingerprint only —
      never by command-line class, so dev and packaged instances can coexist.
      The fingerprint's start time is a DOWNGRADE-SAFE pair: the unversioned
      `start_time` field keeps the legacy `ps -o lstart=` spelling an N−1
      reader still compares correctly (a rollback that met an unknown token
      here would mismatch every row and prune it WITHOUT a kill, orphaning the
      process forever), while the versioned `start_time_boot` sibling — which
      the current reader prefers — carries the Linux `/proc` boot-qualified
      token `"<ticks>.<boot_hex>"` (boot-relative ticks recur across reboots,
      and a bare tick plus a recycled pid is a real collision). The mint order
      for the boot sibling is boot-qualified first; then, when the boot id is
      unreadable, the `ps` wall-clock token (identical to the legacy field, so
      the sibling is omitted); and only once `ps` has ALSO failed, `"<ticks>."`
      as a disclosed last resort (two of THOSE from different boots do
      string-match, which is why they are last, not first). One `ps` is paid
      per SPAWN for the legacy field (a host without `/proc`, or with an
      unreadable boot id, pays it twice — the preferred-token mint re-derives
      the same spelling); the sweep's hot
      path stays subprocess-free, and a SIBLING-FREE mismatched row falls back
      to one `ps` comparison of the legacy spelling before pruning, so a
      mid-generation boot-id readability change never prunes a live owned row.
      A row that DOES carry a boot sibling is judged by boot evidence alone: a
      mismatched sibling is positive proof of another boot and prunes without
      consulting the legacy spelling (whose `ps`-less degradation to bare
      ticks would otherwise re-qualify a cross-boot recycled pid). A recorded
      BARE tick (the pre-change ps-failed fallback) never authorizes a kill
      when any boot evidence contradicts it: on a `ps`-capable host it
      compares against the `ps` spelling, fails, and is pruned — the safe
      direction — while the one host class that ever mints bare ticks (no
      usable `ps`) still resolves PRE-UPGRADE sibling-free rows through the
      legacy helper's own tick fallback (the disclosed one-migration-window
      residual on that class, alongside the `"<ticks>."` cross-boot twin).
      Current-generation session processes take a cheap liveness check instead
      (the cheap path only ever keeps: an alive-but-recycled same-session pid is
      retained one generation and pruned by the next generation's full
      fingerprint check, never killed); full fingerprints remain for
      stale/foreign records, and a DEAD pid still falls through to the
      service-group evidence rather than being pruned on the spot.
      Genuine `daemon` entries are kept; skill companions (daemon scope,
      `purpose companion:<skill>:<name>`) are the exception (v6.36.2) — reaped on
      owner-uninstall or a foreign generation, **log-only by default**
      (`enforce_companion_reap=False` → `process_would_reap`), fail-safe
      (unknown live-skill set ⇒ keep-all).
      `start_parent_lifeline()` gives our python entrypoints (workers,
      extension runner, claude readonly child) a ppid watchdog that
      group-suicides when the parent dies. Panic layers (`_active_subprocesses`,
      port sweeps, Windows Job Objects) are unchanged complements.

# Build, CI & public metadata (not part of runtime)
.github/workflows/ci.yml     ← Five-tier CI (quick / full / integration / skill smoke / build+release)
.github/workflows/claudexor-platform-gate.yml ← 3-OS delegated-execution gate: fixture lane (fake harness, offline, $0) + live lane on explicit API keys (subscription auth deliberately out of CI — verified by a live local run, D26)
.github/workflows/dependency-graph.yml ← Pin-derived GitHub dependency snapshot for the bundled Claudexor runtime; runs only when the pin or workflow changes on `main` / `ouroboros`, or by manual dispatch
.github/workflows/scorecard.yml ← OpenSSF Scorecard on `main` pushes and weekly; full action SHA pins, read-only defaults, OIDC result publication, and SARIF artifact/code-scanning upload
CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md            ← Contributor Covenant 3.0 community rules and private moderator contact
CITATION.cff                  ← Machine-readable software citation plus the preferred six-author technical-report citation; no institutional affiliation is inferred
docs/benchmarks/evidence.json ← Release-bound non-GAIA projection of public benchmark claims and immutable evidence links; README remains the claim SSOT
site/paper/index.html         ← Canonical technical-report landing page with author/citation metadata, `ScholarlyArticle` JSON-LD, paper links, and evidence navigation
scripts/claudexor_platform_smoke.py ← the platform gate's smoke runner (daemon boot, one delegated no-edit/edit task, containment report)
scripts/validate_scope_receipt.py   ← contributor-facing shape validator for the CONTRIBUTING scope-checklist receipt; a thin CLI over `scope_review_contract.normalize_scope_items` (accepts bare/fenced/embedded JSON via the runtime `extract_json_array`)
scripts/fetch_claudexor_runtime.py  ← release-build fetcher for the pinned managed Claudexor runtime archive (claudexor_runtime_pin.json is the SSOT)
build.sh                      ← macOS build (PyInstaller → .dmg)
build_linux.sh                ← Linux build (portable-Python PyInstaller → .AppImage + .tar.gz)
scripts/build_appimage.sh     ← AppDir assembly plus digest-pinned appimagetool and embedded type-2 runtime
packaging/appimage/           ← AppRun desktop/CLI dispatch and Linux desktop metadata
packaging/systemd/            ← Opt-in native-package user unit and operator guide; the unit starts the existing launcher and never owns restart policy
scripts/build_linux_packages.sh ← Wraps the x86_64 Linux payload into dependency-declaring `.deb`, generic `.rpm`, and RED OS 8 `.red80.rpm` assets, including the inert user unit
scripts/smoke_linux_packages.sh ← Release-gating `apt`/`dnf` CLI + installed-unit + desktop-launcher smoke on Ubuntu 22.04/Fedora plus a non-blocking vendor-registry lane for Astra Linux/RED OS
build_windows.ps1             ← Windows build (PyInstaller → .zip)
scripts/build_repo_bundle.py  ← Builds `repo.bundle` + `repo_bundle_manifest.json` for packaged releases
scripts/run_external_review.py ← dual-lane non-committing review wrapper. The default operator lane reviews the staged tree through the production advisory→triad→scope cycle with resolved production policy. `--contributor` reviews an exact committed target-base..head proposal in a detached checkout, freezes the configured `api_chat`/`agent_session` triad and scope rows with blocking enforcement, excludes advisory, rejects contributor version-carrier changes, and emits a redacted SHA-bound packet. Unprovable or contradictory configured→dispatched→observed binding makes the packet `INCOMPLETE`; a proposal changing the review substrate requires a trusted-target rerun. `READY_FOR_INTEGRATION` is evidence, never merge authority: final metadata and exact landing-tree review belong to maintainers.
scripts/contributor_review_evidence.py ← route-neutral contributor-packet binding: verifies content-addressed prompt/response projections, correlates stable slot ids, records route/model/profile/access/effort/terminal-settlement provenance without presenting requests as observed facts, fails closed on typed execution contradictions while retaining non-identity capability deltas as explicit degradation evidence, and carries full redacted agent-session transcripts into the shareable artifact.
scripts/run_plan_review.py ← operator plan-review tool: runs the SAME plan-review engine as `plan_task` (`ouroboros.tools.plan_review`) over an operator envelope — `--goal`, `--plan` (prose file), `--spec-json`, repeatable `--evidence` locators, optional `--subject-root`/`--drive-root`/`--output` — in an isolated drive root; prints the recorded wave (every slot, validated findings, host aggregate) plus the coordinated output, untruncated. Not part of the runtime gate; review-exempt dev tool.
scripts/cleanup_test_pollution.py ← Dry-run-first cleanup utility for local test-pollution artifacts: known test skill state dirs, stale `__extension_imports`, and accidental `MagicMock`-named repo-root files. Use `--apply` only after inspecting planned removals.
skills/telegram/            ← Bundled owner-only Telegram text/photo bridge plus optional Mini App gateway; seeded disabled until the bot-token and host-permission grants are approved, with bridge/Mini App readiness reported by its own bounded status route
skills/unix_computer_use/   ← Bundled extension skill for supervised desktop observation and input, including explicitly configured remote connections stored in skill state. A disabled or missing active connection fails closed instead of falling back to the local desktop; unavailable platform backends report that fact rather than guessing.
packaging/cli/                ← Packaged CLI shell/cmd wrappers and user-local installer launchers copied into desktop artifacts
Dockerfile                    ← Docker image (web UI runtime)
site/                         ← Public GitHub Pages source (Vite). `site/scripts/sync-assets.mjs` copies canonical `assets/` images into gitignored `site/public/assets/`, and `pnpm build` renders committed `docs/`. Text-first product, paper, and install routes, the reciprocal Claudexor relationship on the homepage and `/llms.txt`, `/install.json`, and the sitemap share the same build; `tests/test_public_site_metadata.py` guards canonical URLs, structured metadata, paper citations, related-software links, install data, asset hashes, and source-to-Pages sync.
devtools/                     ← Tracked operator tooling outside runtime and package discovery; domain-specific architecture and methodology stay with the relevant devtool.
devtools/benchmarks/cybergym/ ← Pinned Level-1 CyberGym launcher, adapter, settings, and methodology; detailed topology stays in the benchmark docs.
```

### Devtools boundary

`devtools/` is tracked operator tooling outside runtime and package discovery. Runtime, web, and release code must not import it; a touched devtool still receives normal repository review. Generated operator artifacts belong in an explicit external output root, never in `repo/` or live `data/`. Domain-specific architecture, launch instructions, and methodology stay with the relevant devtool rather than in this core map. An explicitly sentinel-marked isolated devtool root may suppress ordinary progress-log rotation and hot-store growth warnings because its external reader owns a bounded run-local lifecycle; code owns the sentinel, and the exception must remain narrow.

### Gateway Boundary v1

`ouroboros/gateway/` is the single inbound HTTP/WebSocket boundary for browser and CLI clients. `gateway/contracts.py` owns the active envelopes and endpoint index; `gateway/router.py` owns route collection; `gateway/files.py` and `gateway/host_service.py` keep their separately mounted trust boundaries. `contracts/api_v1.py` remains a compatibility re-export only. Domain handlers translate transport into calls on existing runtime owners and must not acquire a second copy of queue, review, settings, or lifecycle policy. `gateway/owner_settings.py` is the one owner-scoped settings WRITE seam shared by the generic settings POST, the single-decision owner endpoints (runtime mode, auto-grant, context mode, safety mode) and the onboarding transaction; membership is calling `_owner_write_settings`. The settings lock is a precondition of the write (a timed-out acquisition refuses before any precondition or write, instead of writing unlocked), an optional precondition is proved under that lock, and a `CommitBoundary` marks the instant the bytes land so a failure in a LATER step is reported as that step failing — never as "nothing was saved". `saved` is a field on both sides of that boundary: every pre-commit refusal answers through `unsaved_error` with `saved:false`, because once a post-commit failure says `saved:true`, an envelope that merely omits the field cannot be told apart from an old or truncated one.

Frontend requests go through `web/modules/api_client.js`; the JSDoc mirror in `web/modules/api_types.js` and gateway parity tests keep the build-free client contract visible. Extension HTTP routes stay under `/api/extensions/<skill>/...`; extension WebSocket events use the namespaced dispatch contract. The reason for this facade is dependency direction: the UI may change without importing the agent body, and runtime internals may change without creating ad-hoc browser contracts.

### CLI / Headless Boundary

`ouroboros.cli` is a first-class client of the same gateway, queue, scheduler, supervisor, and durable task-result owners as the web UI; it does not implement a second task engine. The parser is the command-surface SSOT. Task-streaming commands reserve stdout for the final answer, patch, result object, or JSONL stream and send human progress to stderr.

The headless task transport is explicit: `POST /api/tasks` admits an ordinary managed root, `GET /api/tasks` returns a non-materializing list projection, `GET /api/tasks/<id>` reads the effective durable result, `GET /api/tasks/<id>/events` performs the archive-aware replay/live SSE flow described under Chat, and `GET /api/tasks/<id>/artifacts/<name>` serves a declared artifact or exact content-addressed outbound chat media. Artifact names are simple filenames confined to `data/task_results/artifacts/<task_id>/`; a stored arbitrary path is not a download capability. External task admission cannot mint child authority: the CLI refuses any `delegation_role` other than `root`, and the gateway rejects caller-supplied lineage, subagent labels, and internal task types; only the internal `schedule_subagent` path creates children. Admission reserves the task id and worker slot first, then persists queue snapshot and scheduled result under one lock; failure rolls back only the token-owned row and returns a loud typed refusal. Attachments are copied into the effective task drive before enqueue.

Workspace tasks default to `memory_mode=forked`; `shared` is rejected for an external workspace. A project-scoped task without an external workspace may retain `memory_mode=shared` as the recorded request, but the gateway materializes it on a forked child drive so project data isolation does not depend on relabelling. `--detach` returns only after durable admission. Stream and poll waiters treat a lifecycle result as terminal only after artifact state has left `pending`/`finalizing`. `ouroboros run` exits successfully only for lifecycle `completed`, execution `ok`, no failed/degraded objective, and no failed/missing/pending/finalizing artifact bundle; `--patch`/`--patch-out` are stricter still — a failed or missing patch, a no-change result, an empty payload, or unfinished artifact finalization is an error, so shell automation never reads "the model answered" as "the deliverable exists."

CLI schedules and skill-manifest schedules enqueue ordinary supervisor tasks; they do not create a parallel scheduler. A blank timezone means the DST-aware system zone. The active schedule digest is task/consciousness context, so recurring work is visible to the same reasoning loop that executes it.

Packaged artifacts ship a tiny wrapper and installer rather than a second PyInstaller runtime. `ouroboros.packaged_cli` locates `repo.bundle`, bootstraps the launcher-managed checkout, then invokes the same `ouroboros.cli` module with the embedded interpreter and canonical app/repo/data environment. Packaged `server` is refused (it would bypass launcher-owned bootstrap). Packaged `run --start` is loopback-only: it starts the outer desktop application when no ready gateway exists and waits for both `/api/health` and `supervisor_ready` before submitting work. A normal Linux FUSE relaunch retains direct `AppRun → launcher` execution; a nested extract-and-run relaunch receives a private temporary base whose marker-gated `AppRun` acts as custodian between the type-2 runtime and launcher, waiting for the launcher, removing the verified extraction and the empty private base, then returning the payload status.

Release builds carry Node and ripgrep. Skill-side Node consumers go through `node_runtime.select_skill_node_runtime()` (bundled-first, health-rollback to PATH), while the four generic process launch surfaces run the opposite policy (`process_interpreters.resolve_process_node`): a healthy PATH candidate stays byte-identical; the bundled runtime substitutes only when the PATH candidate is missing or probe-dead, rewriting only bare `node`/`nodejs` argv and attesting a child-env PATH prepend. Neither preference is mode-gated. Every bundled consumer searches the one `platform_layer.bundled_resource_bases()` chain (explicit `OUROBOROS_BUNDLE_DIR`, frozen process root, interpreter-ancestor roots, source checkout) — the explicit handoff is necessary because server/CLI children run from the managed repo without the launcher's `_MEIPASS`.

The embedded interpreter must never write into the signed application: launcher and packaged-CLI entry processes suppress bytecode before project imports; `launcher_bootstrap.embedded_python_env()` redirects child bytecode and user installs to `data/state/` and clears inherited `PYTHONNOUSERSITE`. `platform_layer.pip_install_target_args()` adds `--user` only for `python-standalone`. Disclosed residual: `data/state/python-userbase` sits ahead of the bundle's site-packages and is never pruned or versioned — an older release's dependency can shadow a newer signed copy until the owner removes the directory manually.


A workspace binding changes the contextual repository used by code/file tools; it never replaces the system repository as the source of BIBLE, architecture, development policy, prompts, native skills, or review governance. `/api/tasks` and project-room promotion share `workspace_admission.validate_workspace_root()`: the path must exist, be the exact root of a git worktree, and remain disjoint from the system repo and data drive under resolved, bidirectional, case-folded overlap checks. A set-but-broken project folder fails loudly rather than degrading into a workspace-less self-modification task. Promotion treats an empty project binding idempotently as a provisioned standalone git repository; a non-empty invalid path still fails loudly.

Direct gateway workspace admission performs a read-only preflight over the real target: git state and bounded porcelain rows, manifests and script commands, curated plus manifest-inferred tool availability (labelled `tools_on_path`/`tools_missing_from_path`, because `shutil.which` measures PATH PRESENCE, not executability), and PATH bootstrap dirs. The full report is `workspace_preflight.json`; prompts and metadata get only the bounded summary. Project-room promotion runs the same probe under an eight-second cap on the event-drain path; timeout degrades to an explicit skipped snapshot.

Workspace completion compares against the captured preflight base, so ordinary task-local commits remain part of the deliverable delta. An acting child's patch is bound to `task_constraint.base_sha`; in a shared tree the parent's own commits move HEAD, so `base_sha` stays the patch base and integration relies on reverse-patch shared-workspace verification. An unborn repository compares against Git's canonical empty tree. Patch capture streams tracked binary diff plus admitted untracked files; scratch-matched, cache/build/junk, oversized or binary untracked blobs, and an incidental lockfile without its manifest change are excluded with reasons. A sensitive-looking untracked credential blocks the patch entirely. `workspace_patch.json` is written for EVERY workspace finalization (including no-change and failed) and records status, identities, patch size/hash/diffstat, included/excluded counts and reasons — the truth source for strict CLI patch behavior; `workspace.patch` exists only for `ready_with_changes`.

Forked and empty task state lives under `data/state/headless_tasks/<task_id>/data`. Forked mode copies `identity.md`, `WORLD.md`, `registry.md`; a non-project task also gets the global `memory/knowledge/` tree. A project-scoped fork omits global topic knowledge but keeps the cross-project Pattern Register. Dialogue blocks, scratchpad, owner mailbox, and task history never cross the boundary.

The child drive is execution state, not the durable handoff. On completion the child result is copied to the canonical task-result root and declared artifacts are rebased into the canonical artifact store (missing source = copy failure; name collisions get deterministic suffixes). Verification-receipt replicas are unioned with exact-row de-duplication before copy-back. When the canonical root's `post_task_synthesis` has a terminal value, both late copy-back and effective reads pass the canonical and child records through the same pure field-custody projection: child-owned acceptance may enrich the result, while parent-owned terminal post-task markers and cost/round/token fields cannot be overwritten by a stale child replica. `memory_export.json` is an explicit artifact containing the child memory snapshot, never auto-merged.

Continuation authority uses an explicit `predecessor_task_id` (`""` = fresh; omission/null refused before lookup, enqueue, or spend). An authored root summary is persisted with its exact source at the result owner; Project child-born text is promoted before child-drive collection, while a non-Project parent-born text wins a later child replica overlay. Main builds a defensive provider-only copy: only oversized `result`/`final_answer` strings cross the inline threshold, resolved from persisted narrative, a bounded legacy authored row, or an explicit source-resolvable gap. The automatic startup injection is a bounded continuation ENVELOPE (not a body copy): every compact terminal fact inherits by copy, the predecessor's operative contract core inherits without its nested `predecessor_authority`, every field is whole-or-pointer against one strict tool-result budget, and sha256/chars ride with their observation moment. Durable `task_results` bodies are the untouched SSOT.

System self-modification, external workspace delivery, and `genesis` remain distinct. Reviewed system changes land through the commit gate. External workspace work leaves the selected target tree and returns its patch/manifest. An acting genesis child builds a durable standalone repository under the configured projects root and also receives `deliverable_manifest.json` (bounded content listing, streamed hashes to 64 MiB, size-only disclosure above). Project-local dependencies are ordinary target work; global/system installation remains runtime-policy reviewed, and `sudo` is always non-interactive.

Startup garbage collection uses the common retention policy and removes a headless child drive only when the canonical parent result is terminal, artifact finalization is terminal, the retention window has elapsed, and the recorded child path matches. Canonical task results, artifacts, durable genesis/project repositories, and explicit memory exports survive — which is why all information required after child-drive deletion must cross the canonical handoff before a task is presented as settled.

### Runtime topology

The top level has two continuity roles:

1. The release-reviewed `launcher.py` owns the single-instance PID lock, packaged bundle bootstrap, server process, desktop/browser presentation, restart signal, and final cleanup. Its source may change through reviewed development, but the currently running packaged launcher is outside the managed repository it supervises; replacing it requires a new application artifact.
2. `server.py` is the self-editable inner runtime: HTTP/WebSocket, gateway, message bus, supervisor, durable queue, and worker pool. Managed repository updates replace this inner body without destroying the outer process that can finish, retry, or roll back the transition.

Native `.deb`/`.rpm` packages add an opt-in systemd user unit — an alternate ingress to the same launcher, not a third runtime role. The unit deliberately has no restart policy because the launcher already owns managed restart, the crash fuse, and panic-to-complete-stop; `KillMode=control-group` lets an explicit unit stop reach the whole tree.

On POSIX the launcher starts `server.py` in a new session/process group; on Windows it creates the process suspended, assigns a kill-on-close Job Object, then resumes (failure to establish Job custody refuses to run the child). The launcher writes `data/state/server_process.json` (PID, pgid, paths, ports, argv, creation time) and, before group cleanup, re-proves that the PID is live, is not the launcher, still belongs to the recorded group, has the expected server/repo identity, and has a matching command line; a stale or non-matching record is removed without killing the unrelated PID.

Holding the PID lock licenses the launcher to reap leftover generations of its own install. That sweep runs at `main()` preflight and at the top of every lifecycle generation — ordered recorded-process cleanup, then stray sweep, then port sweep. A PID is proven only on three live kernel facts: the exact `<REPO_DIR>/server.py` argv token after a python interpreter token, `OUROBOROS_DATA_DIR` equal to this data directory, and `OUROBOROS_MANAGED_BY_LAUNCHER=1`; each proof is revalidated immediately before the signal (descendants captured before the root signal), bounded to three kill passes. Kills additionally require a byte-exact environment source (/proc): on hosts without /proc the sweep is report-only and says so. An unreadable environment, a missing marker, or a different data directory is never a license to kill — those processes are spared and named in the launcher log. Only the fresh generation-loop result refuses to start a colliding server. The sweep is POSIX-only (Windows orphans are already reaped by the Job Object), never runs on panic or window-close paths, and deliberately does not consult the custody ledger (missing ledger entries are the defect being repaired).

Extension children, delegated runtimes, supervised task services, local-model processes, and skill companions add processes beneath these roles. Every long-lived runtime-owned process must enter its custody ledger or group so shutdown, cancellation, panic, and restart can account for it after the originating worker disappears. The owner's external applications are outside this tree by design.

The lifecycle thread removes stale port state, starts the server, follows the actual port file, and waits for health. Exit code 42 requests a managed restart (bundle metadata refresh, dependency sync, new inner body). A dependency install failure pauses five seconds and retries once; a second failure is logged and startup proceeds under the existing crash fuse (asymmetric rationale: silently losing pip failure makes a later ImportError inexplicable, while refusing every offline restart would break a checkout whose dependencies were already present). Five ordinary crashes within 120 seconds stop automatic restart. Panic exit performs full cleanup and terminates the outer process rather than entering that retry loop.

On Linux the browser fallback first checks for `DISPLAY`/`WAYLAND_DISPLAY` before touching pywebview, then calls pywebview's own `webview.guilib.initialize()` selector, preserving `PYWEBVIEW_GUI`; for GTK, `Gdk.Display.get_default()` must also return a live display. A disclosed residual for Qt: after a display variable exists, Qt is trusted without constructing a second `QGuiApplication`, because probing it can cause the crash the check is meant to avoid. When the probe fails, the same launcher still owns and supervises the same `server.py`; only presentation changes — it prints the bound URL, best-effort opens the default browser on a daemon thread, and serves first-run setup through the existing blocking `/api/onboarding` web overlay. A repeated launch that loses the PID lock re-reads the port file, soft-polls health ~10s, then prints the last-read loopback URL and best-effort opens it with a bounded join. Missing Git or failed health on the lock-owning startup reports the cause/log paths and exits nonzero. Browser-mode SIGINT/SIGTERM handlers are installed before the lifecycle thread can spawn the server and only set the shutdown event; if the lifecycle thread dies through the crash fuse without a shutdown request, the main thread performs the same cleanup and exits nonzero. `sys.exit` paths leave PID-lock release to the registered `atexit` owner.

A pre-existing cross-platform residual remains: shutdown admission is not atomic with publishing the newly spawned child. A signal in the short interval between `Popen` and recording the child can leave that process for the later orphan sweep. This is tracked rather than hidden.

### Data layout (`~/Ouroboros/`)

```
~/Ouroboros/
├── repo/              ← Agent's self-modifying git repository
│   ├── server.py      ← The running server (kept in sync via the launcher-managed git clone)
│   ├── ouroboros/      ← Agent core package (see §1 tree)
│   ├── supervisor/     ← Supervisor package (see §1 tree)
│   ├── web/            ← Web UI files (SPA + web/modules/* ES module pages)
│   ├── docs/           ← ARCHITECTURE.md (this document), DEVELOPMENT.md, CHECKLISTS.md,
│   │                      CHECKLISTS_ARCHIVE.md, CREATING_SKILLS.md, DESIGN.md, DEPLOYMENT.md
│   └── prompts/        ← System prompts (SYSTEM.md, SAFETY.md, CONSCIOUSNESS.md)
├── data/
│   ├── settings.json   ← User settings (API keys, models, budget)
│   ├── task_results/
│   │   ├── artifacts/<task_id>/   ← Canonical task artifacts + .artifact_manifest.json (private
│   │   │                             provenance) + .scratch_manifest.json (declared scratch
│   │   │                             fingerprints for patch exclusion)
│   │   └── artifact_versions/<task_id>/ ← Non-manifest recovery history for overwritten
│   │                                    user-visible deliverables (last 5 per name)

│   ├── task_trees/<root>/blackboard.jsonl ← Task-tree coordination ledger (ephemeral, GC-eligible)
│   ├── state/

│   │   │                          cost=None and a numeric reservation is counted at that bound
│   │   │                          as unresolved; a zero-usage HTTP-200 body-error (429/5xx in
│   │   │                          body) settles at confirmed $0 so its bound is released

│   │   ├── deep_self_review_context.json ← Last deep self-review Atlas manifest + model metadata

│   │   ├── evolution_metrics_cache.json / evolution_campaign.json / evolution_checkpoints.jsonl
│   │   ├── post_task_evolution_request.json ← Durable promotion signal (worker-written; supervisor
│   │   │                                      idle tick consumes; DROPPED when the owner-stop
│   │   │                                      sentinel is set — a stop is never silently undone)
│   │   ├── post_task_evolution_counter.json ← Per-drive task counter for the every_n cadence
│   │   ├── scheduled_tasks.json ← Queue-backed schedules (cron+tz or one-shot; agent-registerable,
│   │   │                          one-shots fire once then marked done; receipts age out)
│   │   ├── claudexor_rotation_provisioning.json ← Receipt of the last rotation-reconcile POST
│   │   ├── projects.json ← Project registry: immutable id/chat identity, working-folder facts,
│   │   │                   lifecycle/tombstone, routing fence, visible revision; tombstones
│   │   │                   durable and never age-pruned
│   │   ├── project_task_bindings.json ← Task→project bindings (schema v1) with a REQUIRED typed
│   │   │                    origin: ingress-captured source-row ref (+source_text, retention-proof
│   │   │                    full copy, stored only for CROSS-thread origins) or a closed-enum
│   │   │                    origin_absent reason. Immutable except ONE-WAY enrichment; one root
│   │   │                    belongs to at most one Project; tombstoning never removes the binding
│   │   ├── ui_preferences.json ← Owner-local layout prefs + monotonic project_seen_revision ACKs
│   │   ├── queue_snapshot.json ← Durable PENDING/RUNNING recovery projection + worker/reaping/idle
│   │   │                         counts + worker_pool_disabled_reason (typed crash-storm cause)

│   │   │                              outbox; BOTH drop shapes disclosed, never silent)

│   │   │             until a settled successful cycle ACKs; malformed rows stay visible as gaps

│   │   │         install.lock

│   │         bound; advisory_result records optional raw/session metadata while tri-model findings
│   │         stay authoritative), owner_attestation.json (OWNER-STATE; the agent can never forge it),
│   │         review_history.jsonl (append-only full terminal history; new markers declare
│   │         physical_attempt_v1 exact-attribution availability, legacy rows stay honestly
│   │         unattributable), accepted_rebuttals.json, deps.json, auto_repair.json,
│   │         ouroboroshub.json (publication receipt; malformed reads project published=null + a
│   │         typed diagnostic), health.json, auth_token.json, extension_calls/,

│   │                runs, ouroboros-owned.json, daemon.log) — never the operator's ~/.claudexor
│   ├── memory/
│   │   ├── identity.md     ← Agent's self-description (persistent)
│   │   ├── scratchpad.md / scratchpad_blocks.json ← Working memory (FIFO, max 10)
│   │   ├── dialogue_blocks.json ← Block-wise consolidated chat history
│   │   ├── dialogue_summary.md ← Retired legacy flat summary (read-only fallback, not migrated)
│   │   ├── dialogue_meta.json  ← Consolidation metadata (offsets, counts)

│   │   ├── identity_journal.jsonl / scratchpad_journal.jsonl / knowledge_journal.jsonl /

│   │   │   grade history with old/new hashes and content refs

│   │   └── registry.md              ← Source-of-truth awareness map

│   ├── observability/

│   │         redacted tails plus private blob refs

│   │   │                     into Main/Project lenses)

│   │   │             the #198 refusal row doubles as the picker's decision-card authority);
